國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月薪資年增 3.8%。 ⚫ 9 月消費者信心下降 0.1 點至 35.4。 
短評 ⚫ 雖然通膨預期回升與物價壓力抑制部分消費信心 ， 但薪資與所得成長仍持續支撐家庭支出，使消費者信心維持溫和復甦趨勢。

國 
歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 中國 9 月外匯存底較 8 月減少 381 億美元至 3.4 兆美元。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國財政部宣布動用 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務額度，資金將優
先用於「六網」基礎建設等重點項目，加強穩增長措施。 
短評 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。

⚫ 世銀認為人工智慧 （AI） 順風仍足以抵銷能源風險，但警告亞洲地區經

影響，此外，能源震撼延續到明年，補貼政策恐難以為繼。

灣 
出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月消費者物價指數年增 2.73%。 ⚫ 9 月出口額為 872.2 億美元。 
重要
事件

產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達、鴻海及旗下相關企業等遭
列名，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 9 月出口續創佳績，反映 AI 相關需求及科技產品拉貨動能強勁，外需
表現維持熱絡；9 月 CPI 創近兩年半新高，主要受天候因素及能源價格
影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.12 
一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助 ； 若最終認定違反337 條款 ， 涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制 ，

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

## Document 066: 2026年9月宏观数据点评：9月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月宏观数据点评：9月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
9 月宏观数据总体改善，三季度经济增长动能回升 
——2026 年 9 月宏观数据点评 
首席宏观分析师 王青  研究发展部执行总监 冯琳 
事件：据国家统计局2026 年10 月19 日公布的数据，2026 年三季度GDP 同比增速为4.5%，二季度为
4.3%，前三季度 GDP 增速为 4.6%，去年全年 GDP 增速为 5.0%；9 月规模以上工业增加值同比实际增速为
5.5%，8 月为5.2%，1-9 月为5.3%，去年全年为5.9%；9 月社会消费品零售总额同比增速为1.1%，8 月为
0.4%，1-9 月为 1.1%，去年全年为 3.7%；1-9 月全国固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，前值为-7.2%，去
年全年为-3.8%。 
基本观点： 
总体上看，主要受出口增速大幅加快，消费增速改善及投资降幅收窄带动，三季度经济增速企稳回
升，新旧动能转换持续推进。9 月工业生产加速，主要受采矿业以及电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加
值增速回升拉动，当月制造业增加值增速受“中秋错期”扰动小幅放缓，但仍处较快增长水平。前三季
度，受内需走弱、部分行业限产影响，工业增加值增速较上年总体有所回落，但在出口 增速高位上行、
高技术产业增加值两位数增长带动下，三季度工业生产增速较二季度明显加快，是当季经速增速上行的
主要推动力量。消费方面，9 月社零增速加快，主要受去年同期基数走低，“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零
同比表现形成正向拉动，以及电子产品、石油制品等商品涨价影响。受上年同期基数下行等影响，三季
度社零增速较二季度有所上行，但当前消费偏弱局面仍有待改善。投资方面，1-9 月固定资产投资同比增
速为-7.2%，降幅与前值持平，三大投资板块增速为“两降一升”。其中，1-9 月基建投资同比降幅略有
扩大，但在稳增长政策发力支撑下，9 月当月投资降幅收窄；9 月8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投放，
1-9 月制造业投资同比降幅较前值收窄；最后，三季度房地产市场延续调整，1-9 月房地产投资同比降幅
扩大至-20.6%，是当前整体投资负增长的最大拖累因素。 
往后看，四季度出口仍然有望保持 20%以上的高增速，9 月末以来系列稳增长政策出台后，四季度投
资增速有望止跌回稳，楼市调整幅度将会收敛，低基数下居民消费增速也将有所回升。这将带动四季度
工业生产增速上行，服务业生产增速也有望加快。初步估计，四季度 GDP 增速将加快至 4.7%左右；全年
GDP 增速将在 4.6%附近，落在“4.5%至 5.0%”的目标范围之内。我们预计，着眼于稳定明年一季度宏观
经济运行，后期包括全面降息在内，总量型稳增长政策还有发力空间。

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
   具体解读如下： 
一、三季度 GDP 增速企稳回升，背后主要是当季出口增速高位上行，外需对经济增长的拉动力增强，
同时，三季度商品消费增速也有所加快，投资降幅略有收窄。这带动当季工业生产显著加快，新旧动能
转换提速。在系列稳增长政策出台后，预计四季度 GDP 增速将进一步回升至 4.7%左右，完成全年经济增
长目标已没有悬念。 
三季度 GDP 增速达到 4.5%，较二季度加快 0.2 个百分点，呈现企稳回升势头。背后的主要推动因素
是三季度出口增速加快。以美元计价，当季出口同比增长28.4%，较二季度加快 8.3 个百分点，并带动当
季贸易顺差同比扩大 27.2%，较二季度上升 26.4 个百分点，意味着三季度净出口对经济增长的拉动力显
著增强。此外，三季度国内投资降幅有所收窄，居民商品消费增速改善，也对经济增长动能转强有一定
支撑作用。供给方面，三季度出口高增叠加 以高技术制造业为代表的新动能领域增长势头强劲， 共同带
动当季工业生产增速达到5.1%，较二季度加快0.4 个百分点，是当季GDP 增速加快的主要推动力量。 
三季度宏观经济的主要亮点在于，在 AI 投资热潮推动下，当季高技术制造业和现代服务业增速高位
上行，新旧动能转换加 快，整体就业市场保持稳定 ，物价水平温和回升。数据显示，三季度代表整体经
济物价水平的 GDP 平减指数同比为 1.5%，连续两个季度保持正增长。这意味着此前的物价低迷局面得以
扭转，有助于宏观经济走出“物价负增长——经济增长动能减弱”的负向循环。 
往后看，四季度出口仍然有望保持 20%以上的高增速，9 月末以来系列稳增长政策出台后，四季度投
资增速有望止跌回稳，楼市调整幅度将会收敛，低基数下居民消费增速也将有所回升。这将带动四季度
工业生产增速上行，服务业生产增速也有望加快。初步估计，四季度 GDP 增速将加快至 4.7%左右；全年
GDP 增速将在 4.6%附近，落在“4.5%至 5.0%”的目标范围之内。我们预计，着眼于稳定明年一季度宏观
经济运行，后期包括全面降息在内，总量型稳增长政策还有发力空间。 
二、9 月工业生产加速，主要受采矿业以及电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加值增速回升拉动，当
月制造业增加值增速受“中秋错期”扰动小幅放缓，但仍处较快增长水平。前三季度，受内需走弱、部
分行业限产影响，工业增加值增速较上年总体有所回落，但在出口 增速高位上行、高技术产业增加值两
位数增长带动下，三季度工业生产增速较二季度明显加快。 
9 月规上工业增加值同比增长5.5%，增速较上月加快0.3 个百分点，工业生产总体延续回升态势。分
门类看， 9 月采矿业增加值同比增长 1.3%，增速较上月回升 2.7 个百分点，是当月工业生产增速加快的
主要拉动项。采矿业生产增速回正，主要受两方面因素带动：一是煤矿复产稳步推进，9 月规上原煤产量
同比下降 6.8%，降幅较上月收窄 0.9 个百分点；二是原油生产提速，9 月规上原油产量同比增长 2.2%，

### Page 3

东方金诚宏观研究

3 
增速较上月加快 1.8 个百分点。9 月电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业增加值同比增速也有所加快，从上月
的 4.9%回升至5.6%，主要与去年同期基数走低有关。 
作为工业最大门类，9 月制造业增加值同比增长6.0%，增速较上月小幅回落0.1 个百分点，但仍处较
快增长区间。当月制造业生产较快增长，主要受三方面支撑：一是出口保持高增，9 月规上工业企业出口
交货值同比增长 17.0%，增速较上月加快 5.9 个百分点，对生产的拉动进一步增强。二是高技术制造业延
续两位数增长，当月增加值同比增长 15.3%，前值 16.7%。三是国内稳增长政策发力，带动投资、消费下
行压力边际缓和。需要说明的是，9 月制造业增加值增速小幅回落主要受中秋假期错期扰动——今年中秋
落在 9 月、去年在10 月，导致今年9 月工作日比上年同期少1 天，并不意味着制造业景气走弱。 
累计看，1-9 月规上工业增加值同比增长5.3%，增速较上年全年放缓0.6 个百分点，较上半年小幅放
缓0.1 个百分点。今年以来工业生产增速有所回落，一方面受内需走弱拖累，另一方面“反内卷”持续推
进，煤炭等行业限产，也对工业生产形成一定下拉。不过，与需求端投资、消费相比，今年以来工业生
产仍维持较快增长，宏观经济“供强需弱”特征较为明显。这背后有两大支撑： 
一是出口持续高增，外需对工业生产的拉动作用不断增强。1-9 月规上工业企业出口交货值同比增长
10.7%，增速较上年全年加快 8.5 个百分点，较上半年加快 1.0 个百分点。二是在市场供需两旺、财政金
融政策支持力度持续加大的有利环境下，1-9 月高技术产业增加值同比增长 14.3%，增速较上年全年加快
4.9 个百分点，其中，三季度同比增速为16.3%,二季度为 14.0%。目前高技术制造业在规模以上工业生产
中的占比在17%左右，能够对整体工业生产形成较为强劲的拉动作用。 
值得一提的是，三季度工业生产较二季度有所加快，是推动 GDP 增速上行的主要推动力量。数据显
示，当季工业增加值同比增速为 5.1%，较二季度加快 0.4 个百分点，主要受出口大幅走强以及高技术产
业生产加速拉动。 
展望四季度，出口有望保持高增，国内稳增长政策发力将带动投资改善， 居民消费增速也将有所回
升，叠加高技术制造业生产将延续较快增长，以及去年同期基数走低，预计工业生产增速总体上将较三
季度进一步加快。考虑到“中秋错期”对工业生产的扰动将在 10 月消退，当月工业增加值增速有望加快
至 5.7%左右。 
三、9 月社零增速加快，主要受去年同期基数走低，“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零同比表现形成正向
拉动，以及电子产品、石油制品等商品涨价影响； 受上年同期基数下行等影响，三季度社零增速较二季
度略有上行，但当前消费偏弱 局面仍有待改善。 背后是受补贴政策退坡、房地产市场持续 调整、居民消

### Page 4

东方金诚宏观研究

4 
费信心偏弱等因素叠加影响 ，同时，二季度以来 城镇居民人均可支配收入中位数同比增速 放缓，也是近
期消费增速仍然偏低的重要原因。 
9 月社零同比增长1.1%，增速较上月加快0.7 个百分点，分项中，商品零售和餐饮收入同比分别增长
0.9%和 1.2%，增速较上月分别加快 1.6 和 0.6 个百分点。当月社零增速回升，背后主要是去年同期基数
走低，以及“中秋错期”对今年9 月社零同比表现形成正向拉动。同时，从环比看，9 月社零季调环比增
长 0.35%，明显好于上月的下降 0.16%，显示消费增长动能边际改善，但仍低于过去十年同期 0.83%的平
均增长水平，消费偏弱局面仍有待逆转。 
从限额以上商品零售额看，汽车销售下滑继续对社零整体增速产生较大拖累。受新能源车购置税免
税政策退坡、前期以旧换新政策透支居民购车需求等因素影响，9 月汽车零售额同比下降 21.2%，降幅较
上月扩大2.7 个百分点。剔除汽车后，9 月除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长3.9%，明显高于当月社零
整体增速。 
从其他商品来看，9 月限额以上各类别商品零售额增速普遍加快，主要受基数走低及“中秋错期”影
响。值得一提的是，尽管去年同期基数有所走高，9 月通讯器材零售额同比增速仍较上月加快3.8 个百分
点至 31.1%，石油及制品零售额同比降幅较上月收窄 6.2 个百分点至-0.8%，这主要受相关商品价格上涨
拉动。不过，9 月家电零售额同比增速较上月放缓 0.4 个百分点至 1.9%，金银珠宝、体育娱乐用品、家
具、建筑装潢材料零售额延续负增长，尽管降幅与上月相比有不同程度收窄，这主要受补贴政策拉动效
果减弱、房地产市场持续下行、居民消费信心偏弱等因素叠加影响。 
累计看，1-9 月社零同比增长 1.1%，增速较上年全年放缓 2.6 个百分点，较 1-6 月放缓 0.2 个百分
点。从当季表现看，三季度社零同比增长 0.7%，较二季度 0.2%的增速有所反弹，主要是受上年同期基数
下行影响。同时，1-9 月服务零售额累计同比增长5.0%，增速较上年全年回落0.5 个百分点，较1-6 月回
落 0.3 个百分点。需要指出的是，受“中秋错期”影响，9 月当月服务零售额同比增长约5.8%，增速较8
月大幅加快1.6 个百分点。 
今年以来，尤其是二季度以来，社零表现走弱，服务消费增速亦有所走低，主要与补贴政策退坡、
房地产市场延续下行等因素有关。其中，房地产市场下行是主要拖累，这不仅直接抑制涉房消费，也在
持续影响居民消费信心。数据显示，8 月消费者信心指数录得89.5，仍明显低于100 的中值水平。同时值
得关注的是，今年 1-9 月城镇居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 4.5%，增速较一季度和上半年分别加快
0.3 和 0.1 个百分点，但人均可支配收入中位数同比增速较一季度和上半年分别放缓0.5 和 0.1 个百分点

### Page 5

东方金诚宏观研究

5 
至 3.9%。收入均值与中位数增速背离，表明整体收入改善主要受人工智能及有色金属等少数行业居民收
入大幅上行带动，其他多数中低收入群体收入改善相对滞后。这也是二季度以来消费增速总体放缓的重
要原因。 
展望后续，接下来财政金融协同促消费政策有望进一步加力，各地财政部门会出台更多促消费“自
选动作”，国内促消费政策力度有望加大，加之上年同期低基数影响，四季度社零同比有望加速。我们
也认为，四季度加发5500 亿地方政府债券，其中3000 亿用于提高县区一般公共预算保障能力，也有助于
推动四季度消费增速加快。其中，10 月“中秋错期”因素拉动作用退去，社零增速有可能会小幅放缓，
但仍有望保持在1.0%左右，而在低基数带动下，11 月和12 月社零同比增速将加速至2.0%附近。 
四、投资：1-9 月固定资产投资同比增速为-7.2%，降幅与前值持平，三大投资板块增速为“两降一
升”。其中，1-9 月基建投资同比降幅略有扩大，但在稳增长政策发力支撑下，9 月当月投资降幅收窄；
9 月 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投放，1-9 月制造业投资同比降幅较前值收窄；最后，三季度房地
产市场延续调整，1-9 月房地产投资同比降幅扩大至-20.6%，是当前整体投资负增长的最大拖累因素。 
分季度来看，三季度固定资产投资同比增速为 -10.4%，降幅略小于二季度的-11.1%。数据显示，三
季度三大投资板块中，除房地产投资降幅扩大外，基建投资、制造业投资降幅均较二季度有所收窄。 
基建投资方面，1-9 月投资增速为-4.2%，降幅较前值扩大 0.2 个百分点；据我们测算，三季度基建
投资同比下降 7.9%，二季度下降 8.2%。其中，9 月当月基建投资同比下降 5.4%，降幅较上月收窄 1.5 个
百分点。我们认为，9 月当月基建投资降幅收窄，背后的主要推动因素包括：9 月国家发改委等部门加力
推动“六张网”等重点项目建设；当月地方政府专项债发行 6200 亿，为年内最高水平，其中用于化债的
资金规模为 1800 亿，占比明显下降，用于项目建设的资金规模为4400 亿，创年内最高——这或表明，9
月化债对项目资金的挤占效应显著减弱，基建投资动能增强；最后，9 月 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具
启动投放，也对当月基建投资形成较强拉动作用。 
往后看，伴随9 月末央行宣布降低PSL 利率，将“六张网”建设纳入支持范围，加之四季度财政支出
和各类政府债券资金使用赶进度，特别是 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具在四季度全面推进，以及加发的
5500 亿地方政府债券中将有2500 亿用于项目建设，这些都将推动“六张网”和重大项目建设进度显著加
快。我们判断，年底前基建投资增速有望由负转正。这是接下来“加大逆周期调节力度”的主要体现，
也会成为推动今年投资“止跌回稳”的主要拉动因素。 
制造业投资方面，1-9 月累计同比增速为-2.1%，降幅较前值收窄 0.2 个百分点。据我们测算，三季
度制造业投资同比下降 3.8%，二季度为同比下降 3.9%。其中，9 月制造业投资同比下降 0.9%，降幅较 8

### Page 6

东方金诚宏观研究

6 
月显著收窄 5.2 个百分点，主要原因是当月稳投资政策发力，特别是8000 亿新型政策性金融工具开始投
放，其中部分资金用于芯片制造等先进制造业投资。另外，上年同期投资基数大幅下行，也会推动三季
度制造业投资同比数据改善。需要指出的是，受政策倾斜叠加市场供需两旺驱动，当前高技术产业投资
处于较快正增长状态，1-9 月同比增速达到4.9%。其中，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制
造业投资分别增长12.5%、7.6%。 
往看后，着眼于推动投资止跌回稳，接下来 8000 亿新型政策性金融工具、今年超长期特别国债等支
持的制造业项目投资会加快推进，会对四季度制造业投资有支撑作用。我们判断，1-10 月制造业投资下
行势头会进一步收窄，年底前月度制造业投资同比增速有望回正。 
房地产投资方面，1-9 月累计同比增速为-20.6%，降幅较前值扩大 0.7 个百分点。据我们测算，三季
度房地产投资同比下降26.4%，二季度同比下降23.0%，其中，9 月房地产投资同比下降26.3%，降幅较8
月扩大0.8 个百分点。近期房地产投资下滑幅度扩大，背后是三季度以来房地产市场延续调整。其中，三
季度新房、二手房销售增速都有所下行，70 城二手房房价指数环比跌幅也略有扩大。另外，当前金融机
构对房企融资仍然偏于谨慎。 
9 月 29 日，全国范围内针对刚需购房实施定向贴息的政策出台，预计将对楼市有一定提振作用。不
过，8 月 28 日公布系列房地产新政，主要内容包括推动商品房现售，完善房地产开发贷管理，延长居民
按揭贷期限等，核心是将 “房住不炒”制度化，加快构建房地产行业发展新模式。这有可能在短期内加
大房企资金压力，接下来房地产投资还将较大幅度下行。我们预计，1-10 月房地产投资同比降幅有可能
扩大至21.0%左右。总体上看，年底前房地产投资降幅收窄的难度很大。

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing
Full speed on capacity expansion, backed by a
strong US commitment
Cloud AI demand strengthening, with no signs of slowing
TSMC management remains optimistic of a stronger multi-year Cloud AI mega-trend. Its
optimism is based on its prudent and comprehensive assessment of data centre
construction, power shell build up, component supply, and the financial situation of
Cloud service operators. Its capex target was retained at US$60-64bn in 2026, implying

a meaningful uptick to US$19.4-23.4bn in Q4 vs US$40.6bn in 9M26. Our recent
research highlighted TSMC is further accelerating expansion across N3/N2/A14, and
could lift capex to US$90bn in 2027E and US$105bn in 2028E.
Stronger commitment to  US expansion
TSMC guided the overseas dilution on gross margin to rise to 3-4% in 2027 from a
narrowing to 2% (vs. prior 2-3%) in 2026 given the accelerating expansion especially in
the US. The second fab in Arizona should start mass production for N3 in Q327, slightly

earlier than prior plan. Management also stated that it's in talk with SpaceX/Terafab on
potential collaboration, with key principle being to operate the fabs by TSMC.  As what
we wrote recently, TSMC is also evaluating the possibility to expand outside  Arizona,
with Texas being an option. The greater ambition to expand global footprint and
support reshoring is key in solidifying TSMC's competitive positioning, in our view.
Better Q4 sales guidance, slightly down GM guidance not concerning

Q3 EPS of NT$29.43 was above LSEG consensus of NT$28.67, mostly due to higher sales
and OPM of 58.7%. Q3 gross margin of 66.4% was a bit below expectation of 67%
into earnings, given N2 dilution. Q4 sales is guided to grow 14% QoQ at mid point,
slightly ahead of expectation of ~10% QoQ into earnings, driving full year sales
guidance in USD to grow 44% YoY. TSMC guided greater dilution from N2 and overseas
expansion, which lowers Q4 GM 40bps QoQ to 66.0% at the mid point of guidance. We

view TSMC’s investment case on strong growth at sustainable margins with good
support in 2027  (UBS GMs up 10bps YoY at 66.7% from 66.6% in 2026) with price
hikes to pass on higher costs.
Valuation: maintain Buy and target of NT$3,650
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 2-3% to reflect higher sales even with slightly lower gross
margin assumptions. Our new 2028E EPS is 10% above consensus pre-earnings. We
view resilient risk/reward for TSMC stock at 16x 2027E PE vs 36% earnings growth in

2027 based on UBSe, and expect the stock to steadily outperform the broader tech
sector at least through early 2027, assuming the market volatility persists. Reiterate Buy
rating. Our price target is based on 20x 2027-28E PE with a 26% long-term earnings
CAGR.

Company Description
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading dedicated foundry
service provider, with annual capacity of about 32m of 8-inch equivalent wafers as of end-
2024. It operates four 12-inch wafer fabs, four 8-inch wafer fabs and one 6-inch wafer fab in

Taiwan. TSMC also manages two 8-inch fabs and one 12-inch fab overseas under wholly
owned subsidiaries WaferTech in the US, and TSMC China and TSMC Nanjing in China.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target for TSMC on a PE multiple.
We think TSMC faces a variety of risks, including rapidly changing technology, intense
competition, high capital investment and cyclical demand. TSMC also has a history of paying
stock options to its employees, which could dilute earnings.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the

雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
 雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商 
雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月 ， 為國內前二大NB 散熱模組廠 ， 過去主要生產散熱器 、
散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解決
方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。2Q26 產品應用領域 Server 78%、PC 17%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外水冷業務比重於 1H26 約為 55~60%。

2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，
2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 28.4%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元 
雙鴻 2Q26 EPS 10.41 元，低於元富預估的 13.72 元，獲利達成率 76%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 87 億元，+2%QOQ，+64%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 28.42%，較
1Q26 衰退 1.2 個百分點 ， 亦低於預估的30.5%， 主要是報廢品打消 ， 預期3Q26
毛利率回復上 1Q26 水準，2Q26 EPS 10.41 元。

雙鴻 3Q26 營收 97.91 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，營收達成率 94%，
低於預期，主要是七月營收有遞延認列，故推估基期過高，實際 3Q26 拉貨力
道未如預期顯著，預估 3Q26 毛利率 30%，預估 3Q26 EPS 14.13 元。

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊
業務均樂觀

營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務均樂觀 
2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。
氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～
7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以 AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%
對比 20~30%，TR4 將有機會為 100%全水冷 ， 水冷大趨勢不變 ，VR 9 月起量，
TR3 9 月起量 Compute tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出
貨，Compute tray 水冷產值 3000usd 以上，Switch tray 水冷產值 1000usd 以
上，ASIC 水冷產值高於 GPU 客戶，2H26~2027 年 ASIC 增長可期。

1H26 GB 水冷營收中 Rack manifolds 佔比 30-35%、Cold Plate+inner manifolds
佔比 55-60%、CDU 5% ，2026 年可視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。
公司展望 3Q26 營收+15~20%QOQ，實際+12.5%QOQ 低於預期，4Q26 營收
QOQ+雙位數，2026 年營收+70~85%YOY，3Q26 毛利率將回到 29.7%以上水
準，2027 年維持正向看法。

維持買進，目標價 1800 元

維持買進評等，目標價 1800 元 
2Q26 毛利率低於預期為一次性因素，氣冷及水冷兩塊業務樂觀，水冷擴張趨勢
明確，故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 55.74 元，預估 2027 年 EPS 90.36 元，
目標價 1800 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

## Document 232: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

## Document 234: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 241: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields
Date:    4 hours ago
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears
and Lowers Bond Yields
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 15 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash

I. Treasury Yields
US Treasury yields fell sharply today, extending Wednesday's retreat from
intraday highs. The 10-year yield dropped 9bps to 5.10%, while the 30-
year fell 10bps to 5.43% (chart).

### Page 2

Even the 2-year yield declined slightly to 4.77% (chart).
Remarkably, this broad-based decline occurred despite two hotter-than-
expected inflation reports. Normally, such surprises would push Treasury
yields higher as investors priced in a more hawkish Fed.
So, what's going on? We think the bond market is focusing on the
sustainability of the AI investment boom. That issue came to the fore this
week as CoreWeave's bonds plunged below 80 cents on the dollar and

debt financing major data center projects also tumbled. A new Gartner
report estimates AI data center operators will need $8.5 trillion in revenues
through 2030 to cover costs and earn healthy returns, versus projected
revenues of "just" $3.6 trillion.

### Page 3

Why does this matter? The AI buildout has become a major engine of US
economic growth. But doubts about future returns are pushing up
borrowing costs, potentially constraining access to capital and ultimately
slowing investment spending.
As a result, investors may be lowering their economic growth expectations
amid concerns that the AI boom could slow, pushing Treasury yields lower.
This reinforces our longstanding view that the earlier surge in Treasury

yields largely reflected stronger-than-expected nominal GDP growth
(chart).
We are nowhere near ready to write off the AI investment boom. Demand
for compute continues to outpace supply, while rising adoption should fuel
further investment. Indeed, just five hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet,
Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle) are expected to spend a combined $892
billion this year and $1.1 trillion next year. Their strong balance sheets and
cash flow generation make their spending plans relatively insensitive to

higher borrowing costs. While heavily indebted AI infrastructure providers
may struggle, we expect the hyperscalers to keep spending and the
Roaring 2020s to keep roaring.
II. Inflation
Let's take a closer look at September's hotter-than-expected PPI report
and what it tells us about inflationary pressures in the pipeline.

### Page 4

(1) PPI for final demand. September's PPI final demand surged 1.3%
m/m, largely reflecting a 10.8% spike in energy prices. However, even
excluding the volatile food, energy, and trade services components,
producer prices still rose 0.5%, suggesting that inflationary pressures
remain broad-based.
On a y/y basis, core PPI final demand rose to 5.0%, with services and
goods prices rising 5.0% and 5.2%, respectively (chart).
(2) PPI for consumption. The PPI for personal consumption, a key input

into the Fed’s preferred PCED inflation gauge, surged by 1.3% m/m in
September (chart). Excluding food and energy, it was up 0.6%.

### Page 5

Core PPI personal consumption rose to 4.6% y/y and remained well
above both core PCED and core CPI inflation, suggesting that upside
risks to consumer inflation remain elevated (chart).
(3) Supercore inflation. The PPI supercore inflation rate rose to 4.6% y/y
in September (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Core PCED. With September CPI and PPI now in hand, most
forecasters expect core PCED inflation to rise 0.3% m/m in September,
which would lift the y/y rate from 3.0% to 3.1%. The Cleveland Fed’s
Inflation Nowcasting model projects 0.28% m/m and 3.05% y/y,
respectively.
III. Booming Economy
For now, the US economy shows no signs of slowing down. Consider the
latest data points:
(1) Jobless claims. Initial jobless claims edged up slightly to 199,000 in

the week ended October 10, the fifth straight week below 200,000. That is
the longest streak since 1969! Continuing claims declined to 1.71 million,
dragging down the 4-week moving average to the lowest level since April
2023 (chart).

### Page 7

(2) Retail sales. September retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, partly driven by a
6.4% surge in gasoline station sales amid elevated pump prices (chart).
Still, eight of the 13 major categories posted gains. Sales at food services
and drinking places, the only services category and a key discretionary
spending indicator, jumped 1.1% m/m, their eighth consecutive monthly
gain. They rose 7.0% y/y, the strongest increase since March 2023.
(3) GDP. Reflecting today's retail sales report, the Atlanta Fed’s GDPNow

model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at 3.6% (saar), unchanged from
October 8 (chart). Consumer spending is projected to rise 3.6%. AI-related
demand is boosting business equipment investment (17.6%), fixed
business structures (7.0%), and intellectual property investment (6.6%).
Final sales to private domestic purchasers are expected to grow 4.7%.

### Page 8

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

## Document 251: AI算力够不够？硅谷喊“供不应求”，华尔街警告“价格崩塌”

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI算力够不够？硅谷喊“供不应求”，华尔街警告“价格崩塌”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 算⼒投资陷叙事分裂：⻢斯克称算⼒严重短缺、回本周期或压缩⾄六个⽉， 2027 年拟新
增 10GW ；⽪查伊确认客⼾锁定 2027 年算⼒极难，需求预测已系统性失效。 Citadel 创始⼈
Griffin 则警告 GPU 边际成本极低，供过于求将致现货价格⾃由落体，折旧模型失效与企业
ROI 收紧⻛险浮现，市场重新定价才刚开始。
摘要：
AI 算⼒投资正站在叙事分裂的⼗字路⼝。硅⾕的掌舵者们以实际订单和极短回本周期为证据，坚
称当前算⼒严重短缺、供不应求；⽽华尔街的顶级资管⼈则冷静地追问：⼀旦供给赶上需求，
边际定价崩塌、折旧模型失效、企业端 ROI 精算收紧，这⻔⽣意的回报还能否维系？
SpaceX ⾸席执⾏官⻢斯克在最新⼀季财报电话会上抛出了迄今最激进的算⼒扩张计划 —— 预计
2026 年底在线算⼒达到 2.5 ⾄ 3GW ， 2027 年再新增 10GW ，并以 " 如果届时上线算⼒低于
13GW ，我会感到意外 " 来定调业绩指引。他描述的现货市场图景是：所有超⼤规模云⼚商和数

⼗家中⼩公司正 " ⽆情地围追堵截 " ，要求租⽤更多算⼒， " 如果我们对所有⼈都说 ' 好 ' ，我们的算
⼒可以出租好⼏次 " 。⾕歌 CEO ⽪查伊（ Sundar Pichai ）同样罕⻅地公开确认，客⼾今天想锁定
2027 年算⼒ " 极其困难 " ，全⾏业都希望⼿⾥的算⼒是当初规划量的两倍。
然⽽， Citadel 创始⼈ Ken Griffin 却在彭博采访中发出警告：GPU 与航空公司⾼度同构，边际运
⾏成本极低，⼀旦供过于求，现货租赁价格将 " ⾃由落体 " ，当前市场正从 " 主题式扫货 " 转向真正
的基本⾯研究，⽽这次检验 " 才刚刚开始 " 。
这场分歧的核⼼，不仅是对 AI 需求曲线的判断差异，更是对 " 算⼒价值 " 本质的根本性分歧 —— 是
稀缺资源的溢价，还是最终将商品化的基础设施？
⻢斯克：回本周期将压缩⾄六个⽉，算⼒需求远未⻅顶
SpaceX 第三季度财报提供了当前 AI 算⼒供需失衡最直观的数据切⾯。截⾄ 9 ⽉的 2026 财年第三
季度，公司营收同⽐增⻓ 161% ⾄ 145.8 亿美元，超出分析师预期约 10% 。其中超级智能（ SI ）

业务单季营收达 76 亿美元，环⽐激增 50 亿美元，成为最核⼼的增⻓引擎。公司净利润从去年同
期亏损 11 亿美元转为盈利 44 亿美元，调整后 EBITDA 同⽐增⻓ 306% ⾄ 88 亿美元。
⻢斯克在财报电话会上描述的商业逻辑直指需求强度：超算基础设施的部署成本回收周期已压
缩⾄不⾜⼀年，⽽⾸席财务官 Bret Johnsen 则表⽰，以现⾏定价⽔平，每⽡价格已⾼于此前讨论
的 30 ⾄ 50 美元区间上限，回本周期不⾜⼀年。⻢斯克的表述更为激进：
" 不排除明年算⼒的投资回收期缩短⾄六个⽉。 "

### Page 2

⻢斯克选择按现货市场定价签订短期合同，⽽⾮锁定⻓期协议，其底层逻辑是对算⼒单位价值
上⾏趋势的判断：
在部署节奏上，⻢斯克透露团队⽬前每⽉稳定上线约 20 万块 GB300 ，明年将延续这⼀节奏并切
换⾄ Vera Rubin 算⼒。他同时确认，将于第⼆季度末完成 44 万台 Vera Rubin 组成的训练集群上
线。 SpaceX 第四季度资本⽀出预计将达 350 亿⾄ 400 亿美元，公司维持今年年底 SI 业务年度经常
性收⼊（ ARR ）达 1000 亿美元的⽬标，并预测 2027 年底 ARR 将介于 5000 亿⾄ 1 万亿美元之间。
⽪查伊：云业务正为前沿 AI 研发让渡算⼒，需求预测模型已系统性失效
⾕歌 CEO ⽪查伊在接受《 Big Technology Podcast 》专访时，提供了⼀个来⾃ AI ⽣态另⼀极的佐
证。他确认，⾕歌旗舰模型 Gemini 4 Argon 发布后 72 ⼩时内消耗了 25 万亿 Token ，是上⼀代
Gemini 3.0 同期的三倍。他援引的内部数据同样具体： Argon 上线四周内，⾕歌内部代码⽣产量

提升 30% ，代码回滚率下降约三分之⼀。
在算⼒短缺的判断上，⽪查伊直接确认了业界流传的说法合理性 ——客⼾今天想锁定 2027 年算
⼒ " 极其困难 " 。他将这⼀问题归结于基础设施建设的刚性时滞：
" 随着 AI 软件持续进步、模型不断优化，每⽡算⼒能产⽣的有效智能价值也在提升，这是算⼒定
价的根本⽀撑。从合同续签情况来看，我们看到的是每⽡美元单价在上升，⽽⾮下降。 "

### Page 3

更罕⻅的是，⽪查伊公开承认了⾕歌内部算⼒资源的优先级排序：前沿 AI 研发是 " 不可谈判 " 的第
⼀优先级，云业务在实际运营中 " 已经在为⽀持前沿 AI 开发⽽放弃短期收⼊，因为我们在做算⼒
上的取舍 " 。这是⼀个 CEO 级别的正式确认，⽽⾮战略展望。
对于 " 过度建设 " 的质疑，⽪查伊的回应是区分时间框架： " 如果拉⾜够⻓的时间框架，这个量级
的建设完全合理。我们处于有史以来最重要的技术的早期阶段，这不像光纤 —— 光纤铺完是暗
的，没⼈⽤。我们是在每天都⾯临产能约束的情况下使⽤它。 " 他并未否认短期内存在局部过剩
的可能，但对⻓期需求的判断是确定性的： " 给⾜时间的话，我们规划的算⼒最终会有⽤武之
地。 "
Griffin ：三⼤陷阱直指算⼒投资逻辑根基
在 Citadel 年度全球市场未来峰会上， Ken Griffin 接受彭博记者 Dani Burger 采访时，从三个维度
系统性地质疑了硅⾕叙事的可持续性。
边际定价⻛险是 Griffin 的第⼀层警告。他将 GPU 与航空业直接类⽐： "GPU 和航空公司⾮常相

似，边际运⾏成本极低。航班上有⼀个空座，哪怕只卖 20 美元，那个座位也是赚钱的。正是这
⼀特性决定了，⼀旦出现运⼒过剩，定价就会⾃由落体。 " 他援引 2000 年电信泡沫中 " 暗光纤 " 的
覆辙作为历史参照 —— ⼤量已铺设的光纤因需求不⾜从未被点亮，电信价格随之直线下跌。在
他的框架中，当前市场的⾼现货价格并⾮需求韧性的证明，⽽是供给仍处收紧阶段的暂时性溢
价。
折旧模型失效是第⼆层⻛险。 Griffin 指出，当前市场存在⼀个异常现象：五六年前的⽼旧 GPU 依
然能收取极⾼租⾦。但他认为这种状态难以持续： " 越来越多的⼈开始担忧，我不能再像 12 个⽉
前那样，对⼀块芯⽚的第七、⼋、九年给予同样的资本化率或收⼊模型。 " 若投资机构仍按 7 ⾄ 9
年⾼残值进⾏折旧贴现，其估值模型将⾯临系统性重估压⼒。
企业端 ROI 精算是第三层结构性压⼒。 Griffin 描述了⼀个与⼤规模资本开⽀叙事相悖的企业侧图
景： " 美国最精明的企业，正在变得⾮常清醒 —— 哪些业务需要为顶尖模型⽀付极⾼价格，哪些

业务可以⽤开源模型以极低成本运⾏。整个企业界都在看着来⾃ Anthropic 和 OpenAI 的账单，
然 后 说 ： 我 们 需 要 想 清 楚 ， 怎 么 确 保 真 的 从 中 获 得 了 ⽣ 产 ⼒ 。" 据 《 华 尔 街 ⽇ 报 》 报 道 ，
Anthropic 在其 S-1 ⽂件中披露，未来计划投⼊ 7500 亿美元算⼒开⽀，且⽀出规模仍在增加。记
者就此追问 Griffin ，两件事之间是否存在张⼒，他的回答简短⽽直接： " 是的。你在提问的时候
就已经回答了这个问题。 "
"数据中⼼从决策到投产有 2 ⾄ 3 年的滞后，⽽我们今天⼿⾥有的算⼒，是 2024 年做的决策的产
物。就算回到去年今天，智能体任务的爆发还没有发⽣。 "

Griffin 并不否认 AI 技术的变⾰性，他表⽰： " 在这个时刻，⼏乎可以肯定存在局部泡沫。任何对
经济的根本性变⾰，通常都与泡沫交织在⼀起。 " 但他强调，这不意味着当下的定价⻛险可以被
忽视 ——" 市场的重新定价才刚刚开始 " 。
分歧的本质：时间框架与定价基础的双重错位
梳理三⽅表述，这场分歧并⾮简单的乐观与悲观之争，⽽是建⽴在不同时间框架和不同定价假
设上的平⾏叙事。
⻢斯克和⽪查伊描述的，是⼀个当下正在发⽣的、由智能体爆发驱动的即时需求冲击 —— 算⼒
⽆论如何堆叠都不够⽤，回本周期以⽉计⽽⾮以年计，现货价格持续⾛⾼。这套逻辑的成⽴前
提，是需求增速的持续性将压倒供给的追赶速度。
Griffin 质疑的，是这⼀ " 稀缺溢价 " ⼀旦供给完成追赶后能否维系的问题。他的核⼼命题不是否认

当前需求的真实性，⽽是指出 GPU 作为资产类别在结构上接近航空座位 —— 边际成本趋近于零
意味着⼀旦均衡被打破，价格发现将以⾮线性的⽅式向下调整。
两套叙事都有内在⾃洽性，但针对的是不同的时间截⾯。当前的市场张⼒，在于投资者需要判
断：算⼒的稀缺窗⼝究竟还有多⻓，⽽这⼀判断对超算基础设施提供商、 GPU ⼚商及相关供应
链的估值影响，将是量级性的差异。

## Document 256: Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d

Source PDF: all report pdf/Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

In Summary 
• France is the hardest hit in the global bond sell-off, mostly due to its own fiscal and political 
weakness. Since the Hormuz crisis began, 10-year OAT yields have risen 150bps (US +115bps, 
Italy +132bps, Germany +83bps) as the prolonged energy crisis exacerbates fiscal concerns in 
a politically fragile environment. Franc e’ fiscal deficits have remained above 5% of GDP for 
four consecutive years. Debt has risen from 98% of GDP in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, and 
the 2026 deficit is now expected at -5.4% against a -5% initial target. 
• A "short Europe" trade amplifies the move and spreads it to the rest of the Eurozone while 
the ECB looks the other way. Foreign banks and non-banks hold 36% of OATs and Japanese 
investors about 5% (EUR137bn), exposing France to the repatriation trade and price-sensitive 
investors. Speculative shorts, hedge -fund asset -swap unwinds and thin liquidity feed a 
negative loop, w ith market depth more than 50% below normal and the OAT spread over 
Bunds peaking at 150bp s. The speed reflects a positioning shock, not a pure repricing of 
fundamentals. Long -term investors are sitting out ahead of the elections, given budget 
uncertainty and the sensitivity of OATs to political fragility. Spanish and Italian spreads are 
about 20bps wider, bank CDS are widening and French and Italian three -month bills are 
converging toward the ECB's marginal lending rate. Fragmentation is far from 2012 or 2020 
levels, but the ingredients are in place. The ECB has not yet intervened and may limit  itself to 
verbal intervention, while continuing QT and pushing through two more hikes (+50bps).  
• Baseline: A watered -down budget, but the fiscal arithmetic stays heavy for France .  
We expect GDP growth of +0.5% in 2026 and +0.9% in 2027. The Assembly votes on the budget 
on 20 and 27 October, and we expect a budget by year -end through ordinances or Article 
49.3, keeping the 2027 deficit at -5.3% of GDP. This should contain fiscal slippage and stabilize 
financial conditions, with OAT spreads in the 120 -150bps range. Higher rates  (+100bps) will 
feed through over the next three to six months, adding EUR25bn (0.8% of GDP) in gross 
interest expenses for households, non -financial firms and the government combined. The 
debt-stabilizing deficit is -2.7% of GDP, which requires savings of more than  EUR80bn, plus 
EUR15-20bn a year to stop social and interest spending from widening the deficit. This takes 
credible fiscal measures (better tax mix, higher retirement age) and growth -enhancing 
reforms. 
• Adverse scenarios: France-centered crisis or Eurozone crisis? The Middle East situation is far 
from contained; oil prices could stay above USD100/bbl for another six months , leading to a 
further global bond sell -off hitting France the hardest, tightening financial conditions and 
jeopardizing deficit reduction. Domestically, opposition parties could take down the 
government or slippage surprises resurface despite a passed budget, tightening conditions in 
the same way. In this context, GDP would stall at +0.0% in 2027 and spread s reach 150 -
175bps. The ECB would talk up and potentially launch repo intervention. In our view, it would 
take a substantial Eurozone -wide contraction (France -0.5% in 2027 ; Eurozone -0.2%; OAT 
spreads at 175 -200bps) for the ECB to tweak or stop QT, launch open market and repo 
intervention and stabilize funding markets (as OATs and BTPs make up 50% of euro repo 
collateral). 
• Looking ahead, the 2027 elections are the next test, and TPI is a late and uncertain 
backstop. With the presidential election on 18 A pril and 2 May 2027 (legislative elections 
likely to follow), we see four scenarios: i) a President with an absolute majority and allies, who 
delivers fiscal consolidation; ii) a President short of a majority who seeks alliances  (our 
baseline, with the 2028 deficit at -4.9% and spreads above 100bps) ; iii) a President short of a 
majority without alliances  and iv) a political and fiscal crisis, for instance from unfunded tax 
cuts or spending hikes, with spreads above 200bps and widespread contagion. Only in t his 
last scenario would the ECB activate the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI). The risk is 
a lack of political will rather than of instruments, and redenomination risk is the tail case.

Toasted, not burnt: France’s bond sell-off  
and the road to the 2027 election

### Page 2

France is the country most affected in the global bond sell-off

Since the start of the Hormuz crisis,  yields on 10 -year French government bonds (OAT) have risen by 150bps  
(vs 115bps for the US, 132bps for Italy and 83bps for Germany), the highest jump among developed economies. 
The feedback loop between rising oil prices and interest rate expectations  has met structurally vulnerable public 
finances whose credibility has been increasingly undermined by growing political fragility since the 2024 dissolution 
of the National Assembly. France’s debt-to-GDP ratio has surged from 98% in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, with 
over 121% of GDP expected in 2027 (vs 83% to 87% in the Eurozone). Meanwhile, the  weakening economy (+0.5% 
GDP growth expected this year)  is leading to a fourth consecutive  year of fiscal slippage : The government now 
expects a deficit of -5.4% of GDP against the -5.1% targeted in the 2026 Budget. With Moody's upcoming review on 
23 October, France is now at risk of losing its AA rating (currently Aa3 by Moody’s vs A+ by S&P and Fitch Ratings, 
i.e. already one notch below); current pricing, if sustained, would even imply a BBB rating.

Table 1: Macroeconomic and fiscal forecasts for France in 2027

Sources: French government, Allianz Research

France is the first victim of the Japanese repatriation trade.  More than other European sovereigns, France's 
investor base is exposed to price -sensitive players. The most volatile group, foreign banks and non -banks, holds 
36% of French government bonds. Domestic banks and non-banks, whose holdings fluctuate less, account for 32%, 
while non -price sensitive investors (domestic and foreign central banks) make up only 32%. Other European 
countries have either a stronger anchor in the domestic market (Italy  with 66% vs 47% for France ) or with foreign 
central banks ( Germany with 26% vs 16 % for France as Bunds as the primary FX reserve vehicle). Among foreign 
holders, Japanese investors play a significant role , holding around 5% of outstanding French government bonds 
(~EUR137bn). As interest rates rise in Japan, these positions become less attractive relative to  domestic holdings. 
At best they are no longer rolled over, at worst they are actively liquidated. In the recent bond sell -off France 
became the first victim of the J apanese repatriation trade, even though it plays only a minor role compared with 
the US as an overweight in Japanese investors' benchmark allocations .

### Page 3

Fiscal concerns, political fragility and investor base have created the vulnerability ; sentiment and positioning 
determine the market reaction. A "short Europe" trade has built up for some time in markets and France is identified 
as the most vulnerable link (Figure 2). A negative feedback loop is now at work. A bearish narrative around political 
fragility and fiscal risk is amplified by very large speculative short positions, sales from Japanese institutional 
investors, hedge fund unwinding asset swap positions and a l imited presence of buyers, with market depth more 
than 50% below normal. In such conditions , even small trades can move prices sharply. OAT yields have spiked 
rapidly, with the 10-year spread vs the Bund widening to a peak 150bps, rising twice as fast as during the Eurozone 
crisis. Realized OAT spread volatility has jumped to the 90th percentile. These market dynamics point to massive 
repositioning rather than a repricing of fundamentals . But as long as a stabilizing force of buyers is missing, this 
volatility can persist.

Figure 2: A Short Europe trade has built up with pressure on EGB spreads and EUR -USD

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

As c ontagion has switched side s, the European Government Bond (EGB) market could move from country -
specific fragility to general fragmentation. The OAT sell-off has already spilled over to other EGBs. Unlike 15 years 
ago, when France fell victim to contagion from the periphery, it is now the other way round. Spanish and Italian 
bond yields rose significantly, while German yields fell. Their 10-year spreads versus the Bund widened by around 
20bps. This decoupling within the EGB space is accompanied by widening bank CDS spreads, especially in France. 
Meanwhile, Euro funding market is under elevated stress. French and Italian three-month bills are converging 
toward the ECB's Marginal Lending Facility rate, the effective ceiling for Eurozone money-market rates. A break 
above the MLF would signal a disruption in funding  markets. The ingredients for a broader fragmentation of 
monetary conditions are thus in place, even if the current magnitude  remains far from those of  2012 or 2020. 
However, monetary transmission is also a function  of time: If these stretched conditions persist for an extended 
period, an intervention of the ECB will be more likely , e specially if we see a persistent decoupling of yield 
movements between core and periphery that can feed fears of redenomination risk.

What’s next?

The 2027 budget bill is facing stiff opposition at the National Assembly, putting the planned deficit reduction in 
jeopardy. Presented to the cabinet on 1 October, the draft budget (PLF) and the social security finance bill (PLFSS) 
for 2027 target a public deficit of -5.0% of GDP, down from -5.4% in 2026. The budget is essentially about preventing 
the deficit from getting worse rather than a fiscal adjustment. The government has announced  EUR43bn of new 
fiscal-measures (1.4% of GDP) , split between EUR25bn (58%) in spending restraint (including slowing spending 
compared to expected trends such as healthcare spending, central government cuts, social spending restraint, local 
authorities cuts) and EUR18bn (42%) in new revenues.

First, opposition parties are already pushing back against most of the big revenue raisers measures, including the

### Page 4

cut to the tax allowance on pensioners’ income (EUR1.4bn), the taxation of sick leave (0.5bn) and cutbacks on 
funding to local governments (EUR2.5bn). Second, the persistence of elevated energy prices and tighter financial 
conditions could pressure GDP growth next year, denting expected government receipts. The government expects 
GDP growth at +1% in 2027, while we e xpect +0.9% but with increasing downside risks. The next key milestones to 
watch will be 20th and 27 October when the National Assembly votes on the Draft budget part I (revenues) and 
the Social Security finance bill (Figure 3). The debt stabilizing fiscal deficit stands at -2.7% of GDP, half of today’s 
level, which entails an improvement of the public deficit of EUR83bn from 2027 on top of around EUR15-20bn of 
additional savings to find each year  just to prevent the deficit from rising  (to contain rising social security and 
interest spending).

Figure 3: France’s key political & budget milestones

Note: legislative elections are not yet scheduled – the National Assembly’s term runs to 2029 unless the next president dissolves 
it. Sources: LOLF art. 45, Constitution art. 47, Assemblée nationale budget calendar of 15 Jul 2026, rating agencies, Allianz  
Research

We lay out different scenarios of what could happen between now and the elections (Figure 4). Starting in the 
short-term, we envisage three broad possibilities: i) a watered-down budget passed before year-end which plans a 
stabilization or a slight improvement of the deficit, ii) a political crisis  with opposition parties taking down the 
government and iii) a re-intensification of the energy crisis.

Figure 4: Economic, political and market scenarios October 2026-April 2027

Source: Allianz Research 
In our baseline scenario, we  expect the French government will manage to pass a watered-down budget while 
the energy crisis eases,  allowing the deficit to remain in check in 2027 (-5.3% of GDP) Opposition parties do not

### Page 5

have an incentive  to soften their stance with the Presidential elections approaching on 18 April and 2 May 2027. 
But they also do not have an incentive to create a political crisis by taking down the government. Notably, the 
National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN) has expressed a preference for implementing a budget rather than 
relying on the ‘’Special Law’’ mechanism that was used in early 2025 and early 2026 In this environment, we expect 
the government to manage to pass a mini budget, by year -end, of limited fiscal effort just to keep the deficit in 
check (at -5.3% of GDP), either through executive ordinances – a budgetary mechanism never previously utilized 
under the Fifth Republic – or by triggering Article 49.3.  
In this baseline scenario, OAT spreads should enter a phase of stabilization but contagion risk remains. After the 
clean-up of derivative positions, we see 10 -year OAT spreads trading in a pre -election range of 120 -150bps. But 
the evolution of the French spread will not be isolated , with the potential to trigger a renewed contagion move.  
The French budget and election situation feeds the “short Europe trade”, and this negative sentiment dominates 
country-specific fundamentals. We also do not believe that stabilizing the French situation alone will be enough to 
bring the EGB market back into a convergence trade. Now that dislocations have emerged, EGB spreads are likely 
to become the new playground for speculative (leveraged) players looking to exploit them. With this comes 
elevated rates and FX  volatility and the risk of money market stress. We expect the ECB in this case to limit itself 
first to verbal intervention like a change in wording from "monitoring" to "ready to act" or "disorderly market 
dynamics". We see the tipping point at a 10-year OAT spread above 150bps. 
In a more adverse scenario where the energy crisis does not ease and/or France slips into another political crisis, 
a crisis emerges, either France-centered, or with spillovers and contagion to other Eurozone countries.  Under a 
scenario of renewed tensions in energy markets, the global markets selloff would intensify. Financial conditions 
would tighten more in France, weakening the economy and putting in jeopardy the deficit reduction. Alternatively, 
the government could be taken down in Parliament, triggering a grave political crisis and leading to tighter 
financial conditions. Even under our baseline scenario of a watered-down budget, fiscal slippages could re-emerge 
– for example if the economy is weaker than expected, or if the fiscal adjustment fall s short of what is planned  
(e.g. if revenue -raising measures disappoint). Additionally, under a Special Law, the risk of wider deficit could 
increase if continued through the presidential elections  in 2027, which could lead to France -centered crisis or a 
Eurozone crisis if tightening financial conditions in France spill over to other Eurozone countries . In a France -
centered crisis we would expect the French economy t o enter a mid-sized recession as tighter financial conditions 
would weigh on domestic demand (see next section) , with GDP at +0.0% in 2027. In a Eurozone crisis , we would 
expect French GDP to contract -0.5% and the whole Eurozone economy -0.2%.  
In this adverse scenario, we could see the 10-year OAT spread rising to 1 75-200bps. This would create 
widespread contagion in the EGB market and in the banking sector. In this case, it will clearly be up to the ECB not 
to let the situation escalate into a euro crisi s 2.0. Unlike in the euro crisis, when the escalation was fueled by a lack 
of instruments while the political will was there, this time the deployment of the instruments (TPI, OMT) could fail 
due to a lack of political will and institutional inertia. It wo uld be particularly precarious if markets began to price 
redenomination risk, i.e. the possibility of a country leaving the Eurozone. But we see this scenario as a tail risk. If 
the EGB and money markets move towards general fragmentation, we expect the ECB to set operational steps (QT 
tweak, temporary operations) at OAT spreads around 175bps, but only alongside broader contagion with BTP 
spreads around 150bp s and Spain and Portugal widening together. For France, a QT tweak would represent a 
relief of EUR4.8bn on average per month, equivalent to around 20% of monthly issuances since June. But the 
problem is that “Europe short traders” can also do this math and adjust accordingly. At this point the ECB would 
not only operate in the secondary bond market but a lso need to stabilize funding markets as in  French OAT and 
Italian BTP represent 50% of the EUR repo market collateral. The TPI activation would be late in the process and 
require OAT spread around 200bps with widespread and persistent contagion.

In 2027, the presidential (and likely legislative) elections  could re -escalate economic and market pressures.  
We envisage four scenarios, which we detail more in Figure 5. In a nutshell, under our baseline assumptions, the

### Page 6

newly elected President will not secure a n absolute majority when she or he provokes legislative elections.  
Nevertheless, the President will have the strongest group at the National Assembly and will benefit from strong 
popular support. In this environment, we would expect the President to negotiate with some opposition parties to 
pass a budget putting the deficit on a downward trajectory. Nevertheless, deficit reduction will be limited ( -4.9% 
GDP deficit expected in 2028) amid strong aversion for rapid fiscal tightening in Parliament and public opinion. The 
10-year spread will likely remain above 100bps. In an adverse scenario of political and fiscal crisis, for instance 
triggered by the enactment of tax cuts and/or spending hikes  not credibly funded, a France-centered or Eurozone 
crisis scenario would re-emerge. In the latter case, the ECB would likely intervene with the TPI with French spreads 
breaking 200bps and Italian spreads above 175bps.

Figure 5:  Economic, political and market scenarios after April 2027

Source: Allianz Research

In the short term, a significant easing of global yields and elevated French spreads hinges on three factors:   
1) a substantial easing of the energy crisis, which would reduce inflationary pressures, limiting or even ending 
further central bank hikes. 2) Strong and credible fiscal -consolidation measures passed by decree, though this 
remains unlikely before the presidential election. One option used previously is  reducing healthcare spending, but 
the amount of savings is limited to EUR5-10bn (Table 2). 3) A cooling US economy could moderate the global bond 
sell off, though this too seems less likely before 2027.

Table 2: Measures the French government can implement by decrees to bypass parliamentary vote

Measure Can government act without new parliamentary vote? 
Cancel already authorized State spending Yes 
Freeze/cancel some budgetary appropriations Yes 
Adjust medicine reimbursement within regulatory powers Yes 
Adjust healthcare tariffs/payment parameters Yes, within statutory framework 
Tighten implementation of existing social schemes Often yes 
Change parameters of social contribution rebates  Sometimes yes 
Abolish/reduce a statutory benefit Generally, no 
Permanently change tax rates No 
Fundamentally redesign social-contribution exemptions Generally, requires legislation 
Change the ONDAM target itself Generally, Parliament/LFSS

Sources: French government, various, Allianz Research 
In the longer-term, only a sizeable fiscal -consolidation program can ease French spreads sustainably.  
Ideally, a combination of the following would have the most benefits:

### Page 7

Credible fiscal adjustment (the least growth -hurting). On the revenue side, fiscal consolidation should rely on a 
more efficient tax mix to limit negative growth effects. An increase of  VAT rates would bring large receipts without 
hurting potential growth. Freez ing income-tax thresholds should be preferred to increase social contributions to 
limit the negative impact on labor demand and labor supply amid already high labor taxation in France. Some 
inefficient and costly tax rebates and loopholes could also be cut down. But most of the fiscal adjustment should 
be operated through expenditure cuts. An increase of the effective age of retirement – for instance through a rise 
in the number of years of contribution – would bring substantial savings and increase tax receipts. Substantial 
savings could also be enacted on public agencies and local governments’ administrative costs. In parallel, the 
government could launch a comprehensive spending review to improve efficiency  of public expenditure, 
benchmarking what has been done in the past in countries such as Canada and Sweden.  
Growth-enhancing reforms. The government could implement structural reforms which will likely support growth 
in the medium -term, reducing the need for further painful fiscal adjustment later on. A pension reform would not 
only bring higher receipts and increase savings; it would also stimulate labor supply and potential growth 1. Other 
growth-enhancing measures include a revamp of school education met hods, scaling back excessive regulation, 
incentivizing higher-skilled education and hiring through a reform of the taxation system.

The trickle-down effect of tighter financial conditions

The ongoing bond market problem could increasingly become an economic problem through tighter financial 
and credit conditions, but the economy has buffers to sustain a short -term financial shock. Higher sovereign 
interest rates have started to filter through on to consumer, mortgage and corporate funding costs, and the pa ss-
through will continue through 2027. This will tighten the credit and financial cycle, through higher borrowing costs 
on new lending, higher refinancing bills and tighter access to credit, and ultimately weaken consumer and 
investment spending. Our BIS-built Financial condition index (FCI) for France shows that overall financial conditions 
have tightened sharply recently, but from a relatively loose level (Figure 6, left). They are now close to their long -
term historical averages. Besides, the credit impu lse for households and corporations remain s supportive to date 
(Figure 6, right). During the Eurozone crisis of 2011 -13, the credit impulse turned sharply negative but GDP growth 
held up. While the latest ECB Bank Lending Survey (BLS) points to the credit impulses weakening in the next couple 
of quarters, this does not necessarily mean that GDP growth will weaken sharply. The French economy has buffers 
to withstand a negative finance shock, provided it does not last too long and does not get worse. In parti cular, 
households have high savings and corporates’ cash reserves have been rebuilt recently, though many firms struggle 
with elevated bankruptcies.

Figure 6: France’s financial conditions index (left); credit impulses & GDP growth (right)

Sources: LSEG Datastream, BIS, Allianz Research 
Note: last data point in the FCI is 5th October 2026.

French households, non -financial corporates and the government. We find that  a +100bps increase in interest

rates would almost double households’ net interest income in 2026 to -EUR16.3bn from -EUR8.5bn in 2025, or the 
equivalent of 0.8% of disposable income (see Figure 5). Contrary to US or UK households, French households tend

Figure 7: Impact of a +100bps shock on interest rates for French households

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

For French non-financial companies, around 50.6% of debt is due to reset or mature within the next 12 months, 
well below the level in Italy, Spain, the UK and the US, where 78 to 90% is due for refinancing. However, France 
starts from the largest corporate debt stock in Europe in this sample, at around EUR2.27trn. This means that even 
with a lower refinancing share, around EUR1.15trn of debt still needs to be repriced within a year, more than in 
Germany, It aly o r Spain. In addition, the pressure is amplified by the starting level of borrowing costs. French 
corporates already face an effective interest rate of 6.1%, the highest among the European peers shown here. Under 
a +100bp s interest-rate shock, the effective pass -through would amount to roughly +89bp s, adding around 
EUR10bn to annual interest expenses. Relative to gross operating surplus, this represents an additional burden of 
about 2.1% of GOS, the highest impact across similar peers, and could shave roughl y -0.7pp off gross margins (see 
Figure 6). For the French government, a +100bps increase in the weighted average interest rates would mean +0.3% 
of GDP in 2027 (EUR 11.7bn) and +0.7% of GDP by 2035. Indeed, France has a rather long average maturity of its 
debt (>8.5 years) which protects it from very high volatility on its interest expenses.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
